   - A -> E -> B -> G -> H
   - A -> E -> B -> T -> F -> G -> H
   - A -> S -> B -> G -> H
   - A -> S -> B -> T -> F -> G -> H
   - A -> E -> B -> T -> I -> J -> G -> H
   - A -> E -> B -> T -> I -> Q -> J -> G -> H
   - A -> S -> B -> T -> I -> J -> G -> H
   - A -> S -> B -> T -> I -> Q -> J -> G -> H

2. **Determine Critical Nodes:**
   - **B**: Removing B breaks multiple paths that go through B.
   - **G**: Removing G breaks multiple paths that go through G.
   - **E** or **S**: Removing either E or S breaks the paths that go through E or S.
   - **T** or **R**: Removing either T or R breaks the paths that go through T or R.

   Removing either B or G alone is sufficient to disconnect A and H, as they are central nodes in the paths connecting A and H.

3. **Conclusion:**
   - The minimum number of nodes to remove is 1.
   - Possible cut sets: [B] or [G]

Answer: Nodes to remove: 1, Cut Set: [B] or [G]

To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the black nodes \( I \) and \( Q \), follow these steps:

1. Identify all paths from \( I \) to \( Q \).
2. Find the critical edges whose removal will eliminate all possible paths between \( I \) and \( Q \).

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify adjacent nodes:**
   - Node \( I \) is directly connected to: \( G \), \( H \), \( P \), \( J \)
   - Node \( Q \) is directly connected to: \( F \), \( B \), \( J \)

2. **Find paths from \( I \) to \( Q \):**
   - \( I \to G \to B \to Q \)
   - \( I \to F \to B \to Q \)
   - \( I \to F \to Q \)
   - \( I \to G \to E \to B \to Q \)
   - \( I \to J \to B \to Q \)
   - \( I \to J \to Q \)

3. **Identify critical edges:**
   - Removing \( (I, J) \), \( (G, B) \), \( (F, B) \), and \( (Q, F) \) or \( (Q, B) \) will break the paths:
     - \( (I, J) \) removes paths through \( J \)
     - \( (G, B) \) and \( (F, B) \) ensure \( I \) and \( F, G \) do not connect through \( B \)
     - \( (Q, F) \) ensures that there’s no direct path from \( I \) to \( Q \) nor indirectly through \( J \) or \( B \)

By removing the edges \( (I, J) \), \( (G, B) \), and \( (F, B) \):
   - Path through \( G \to B \to Q \) is removed by \( (G, B) \)
   - Path through \( F \to B \to Q \) is removed by \( (F, B) \)
   - Path through \( J \to B \to Q \) or \( J \to Q \) is removed by \( (I, J) \)

Thus, there is no way to connect \( I \) and \( Q \) after removing these three edges.
